This means that the school should start teaching and that a regular timetable should be set at the start of the school. ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e11042	Mr President, I should like to thank the President-in-Office of Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen in the House! Trafficking in human beings, especially women, is a major problem worldwide. We from the EIA take this problem very seriously. As Mrs Stadlbauer has already said, trafficking in human beings affects almost 100 per cent of women, women who are mainly attracted to the West for the purpose of sexual exploitation from Eastern Europe. This often involves withdrawal of freedom, injury of the body, withdrawal of passport, torture and cetera. The victims come to about one third from Romania, with other victims coming from Bulgaria, Belarus, Hungary, Slovakia, Lithuania, Moldova and Poland. For years, there have been efforts to counter trafficking in women. A basis for this is the new § 104a in the Criminal Code, which created a general and comprehensive criminal provision against trafficking in human beings. Special measures to protect victims have also been particularly strengthened. In the field of security authorities, it was recommended by the Federal Ministry of Justice that files should be excluded from the file if there is a fear of injury to those concerned or to third parties. So addresses and names of the victims are not available to the perpetrators. Furthermore, in the course of their testimony to loads, the victims have the opportunity not to give their own home address but to mention another loadable address, such as those of victim protection facilities. In Austria as well as across the EU and in the South-East European area, special training offers are offered for police, special agents, judicial officials and consular officials. Transnational strategy and action plans are also being developed in these regions. As mentioned by the Federal Minister, Austria has a pioneering role by setting up a task force for trafficking in human beings. It forms the central coordination body for combating trafficking in human beings involving NGOs. National victim protection strategies have a huge significance in all measures: starting from the provisions already mentioned in Austria, specifically for the surveys of victims trained security officials, to cross-country measures to protect victims and their families in the country. Therefore, the strategies must be comprehensive by having to work together both target countries, transit countries and countries of origin. Finally, I would like to say that trafficking in human beings, and trafficking in women in particular, are inhumane and vehement. The government parties, under Chancellor Wolfgang Schüssel, demonstrate by the measures and cooperation that have taken place in recent years that, while respecting all aspects of fair negotiation and human rights, the protection of victims is always at the centre.

In spring 2001, a poll on chimpanzees provided the picture that 85 percent of respondents believe that chimpanzees have a complex social life and that their intelligence and emotional structure are very similar to that of humans. Nine out of ten respondents consider cages permitted under the Animal Protection Act to be too small, and at least 51 percent – which is a little surprising – think chimpanzees should have the same legal status as children. The last answer, however, also shows that people have a very naïve picture of animal experiments, because I don't think anyone can really believe that these animals survive animal experiments at all. By international comparison, Austria is taking on a certain pioneering role with this ban on animal testing, because Austria – with the exception of Sweden and the Netherlands – is the only country planning a ban. It is therefore only to be welcomed that all parties represented in the Committee will agree to this request. Thank you.

One is animal experiments carried out on nonhuman monkeys, that is to say on other monkeys. I can remind you that in cognitive performance the half monkeys, as they are called professionally, e.g. Rhesus monkeys, pavians, macaces, all possible, are not so different from the apes of man, which is why we should consider taking similar steps and limiting the animal experiments on them, if not even letting go out. I can only give you an example of how these are still “used” by science today, under quotation marks. There is the so-called primate chair. This is a device in which a living animal is clamped, namely all four extremities separately in different positions, the head is fixed, sometimes even fixed with screws in the skull – and then this so-called chair is rotated in different directions and it is often carried out mainly neurological tests, which are often enough combined with water withdrawal, with a drop of water being offered as a reward for a correct response of the animal. So relatively cruel, but this is still being carried out today on Rhesus monkeys and others. Not to mention the many animal experiments carried out on dogs, cats or pigs, so also all the animals to which many people have at least a fairly positive access. I would just like to add a number because Mr Wittauer REACH referred to these 3.9 million additional animal experiments in the room. This is the conservative estimate of the EU Commission itself. It can be assumed that the number is still much higher; perhaps not quite as high as the animal protectors indicate on the other spectrum, namely 40 million, but somewhere in between. The second area I consider very important is that we are forcing the control of the legal provisions that we have decided in Austria to insist on complying with these laws. I only mention two examples where I believe we need action in the next few weeks and months. One is the songbird catch in Upper Austria, which ends in a legal-bureaucratic inverter where the competences lie or not, but which has not prevented tens of thousands of songbirds from being caught again this year. [[Abg. Ellwall: The Greens have prevented this in Bad Ischl!]] And the second is a detail that was regulated in the Federal Animal Protection Act, the prohibition of electric shock equipment in dogs, which are still being used in the hunt today, on the grounds that hunting is not included in the hunting law. This is correct – to my suffering, I say – but what is true is that no person in Austria is legally permitted to acquire or own such a device. And that's not gonna make it possible to use it either. Maybe you'll share this information with all those who have friends and acquaintances who are hunting somewhere. To sum up, I hope that we can continue this path in animal welfare and achieve further improvements.

Point Report of the Committee on Science and Research on the Government Template (781 e.g._Other Organiser Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Austria and the Government of the People's Republic of China on the mutual recognition of equality in higher education (1253 D.B.)
ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e15741	We're entering the 19th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First of all, Mr Kurzbauer. Desirable time: 2 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e15769	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! This agreement between the Republic of Austria and the People's Republic of China concerns cooperation in the field of science and exchange in higher education. Cultural relations with China have developed very positively just last year. That is why this agreement is particularly to be welcomed. The main issue is the mutual recognition of equality in higher education. On the Austrian side, this agreement applies to all universities and colleges recognised by the state and state; on the Chinese side, to all universities recognised by the state and state, as well as to research institutions eligible for the award of academic degrees. The aim of this Agreement is to establish the mutual recognition of diplomas, examinations and academic degrees in certain fields. Ladies and gentlemen, just a few sentences left to the agenda item 17: Mr Moser has already made very critical comments on the field of research and technology, which were then further weakened by Mr Trunk. Ladies and gentlemen, I think that the research landscape itself has developed positively since 2000 and is a success story. This has also been supported by figures in the debate today. Ladies and gentlemen, we are on a good way towards Barcelona goals, 3 percent by the year 2010. This success is partly private investors and partly economic, but in particular thanks to the efforts of our Federal Government. Investments in education, research and development are investments in the future, and investment in the future means competition, safeguarding and creating new jobs. In addition to the regular budgets between 2002 and 2004, the Federal Government has made available €1.3 billion with various offensive programmes. Ladies and gentlemen, the total is 17.8 billion schilling. Allow me to compare the year 1999: In 1999, Austria was in the middle of the European Union in the field of research and technology development, and we are currently on our way to an absolute top place. Ladies and gentlemen, this comparison makes us sure!

ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e17942	Mr Dipl.-Ing. Missethon. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e17964	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! The trigger for today's change of the Motorways Act was basically three problems. The fact that, in accordance with the current legislation, the contracts for additional courses on concessionated power lines are to be awarded to the holder of the concession has contradicted European law, the principles of non-discrimination and transparency. This amendment amends this provision. It is clarified that, in the case of concessions, in the case of public procurement services, the provisions of national and Community procurement rights must in any case be put to the vote. The absence of the provision on non-procedural extension of the duration of the concession is in line with the principles of primary law. The second major focus of this law is also amendments which are not subject to Community law. This is also a first step towards local transport reform, that there has been a definition of responsibilities, that administrative simplification has been achieved by removing the investigation procedures in the case of transit line traffic, and that a clarification of competences has been made. In this sense, we will agree to this law.

In 80 states alone people have died after torture in the last three years. In Austria, but also in many European and non-European countries, torture and other cruel, inhuman and degrading treatment are prohibited in accordance with the European Convention on Human Rights, the UN Convention and other conventions. But if you do this day-to-day thing, Unfortunately, it must be noted that while some countries have officially abolished torture measures, the practice teaches us another. Examples are China, but also the USA with its prison camps in Guantanamo, Afghanistan, or Iraq. The UN Special Rapporteur on Torture and Human Rights, Professor Manfred Nowak, was convinced after years of long and tough discussions with the Chinese government on the current human rights situation in China. After China abolished torture in 1996, he had to find that the use of torture was still widespread. But the situation in Tibet has not improved significantly. Many Buddhist monks and nuns are painted and housed there under the most adverse conditions. Obviously, the US should also have something to hide. The US government refused to allow UN employees access to Guantanamo. Under the guise of the fight against terrorism, fundamental rights in prison camps and suspected prisons are constantly being disregarded and human rights are trampled on. The false belief that, for example, international obligations are not to apply in camps in Afghanistan, but only Afghan law is applicable there and therefore torture is legal, without any basis. Any easing of the ban on torture enshrined in international law is neither ethical nor legal, nor politically acceptable. For Austria, it seems useful to draw up as soon as possible a national approach to the implementation of the already signed Additional Protocol, to ratify it and to use it at European level for EU ratification and implementation in the course of the 2006 EU Presidency.

Point Report of the Committee on Human Rights on the Government template (996 e.g._Other Organiser Protocol No 14 on the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms on the amendment of the Convention's control system (1255, e.g.)
ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e19540	We're on the 27th. Agenda item. First speaker is Mrs Herzegovina. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e19564	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! The European Convention on Human Rights has often been amended and supplemented by additional protocols during its existence. The accession of many new Contracting States to the European Convention on Human Rights, as well as the increasing awareness of the Convention, leads to more complaints to the European Court of Justice every year. In 2004, 80 000 complaints were pending at the end of the year. The Court of Justice expects further 52 000 new complaints for 2005. ==References== Additional Protocol is intended to allow simplified procedures for the appeal groups, and clearly justified complaints to be dealt with in a seven-member body are assigned to a three-member committee. The group of apparently unfounded complaints shall be remitted to the jurisdiction of individual judges. Furthermore, the possibility of forming chambers consisting of only five, rather than seven, judges is also opened up. The term of office of judges shall be extended to nine years without the possibility of re-election. Further improved instruments for the implementation of judgments of the European Court of Human Rights are also being introduced. ==References== However, the additional protocol also provides, above all, the prerequisite for the accession of the European Union to the European Convention on Human Rights. ==References== Additional Protocol for its entry into force of the ratification of the Contracting States. Therefore, it is not yet possible at this point when this entry into force will actually be possible, since, with the exception of Russia, all the Member States of Europe have signed the Protocol to the European Convention on Human Rights, but only 20 out of 26 Member States have ratified it. This Protocol therefore provides for a package of measures to allow human rights complaints to be concluded within a reasonable period of time. It reduces complaints and, above all, contributes to ensuring and strengthening the efficiency of the Court of Human Rights.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e19604	The next speaker was Mr Keck. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e19626	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Through new States Parties to the European Convention on Human Rights, the number of human rights complaints made annually to the European Court of Human Rights has increased considerably. This reform project aims to increase the efficiency of the Court of Justice. The one-off term of office of the judges of nine years – up to now six years have been possible – strengthens the independence and impartiality of the judges. The importance of this is explained by an example from Austria, which I also brought to the Committee. In 2001, a questionable personnel decision to extend the term of office of then judge, Dr. Willi Fuhrmann, took place. The extension was rejected. Willi Fuhrmann became a judge at the European Court of Human Rights in 1998, nominated by Austria. Under the scheme at that time, a term of office of six years was provided for, with the possibility of a one-time appointment. In order to ensure a good mix of judges, it was established that the term of office should be set for half of the judges for three years. The decision was made by Lot, and Willi Fuhrmann was such a judge who had only a three-year term of office through Los. Willi Fuhrmann, however, has acquired an extremely high reputation in his work in Strasbourg with his colleagues and in the professional world and has shown himself to be highly qualified in this function. When the term of office was extended in 2001, the then black and blue government refused to extend Willi Fuhrmann, and other candidates were nominated, which is quite legitimate. In this context, however, it has been publicly stated that this decision was not exclusively based on technical criteria, but was purely party-political, operated by the then vice-president Riess-Passer. A similar questionable, party-politically motivated approach will no longer be possible by the new regulation concerning the term of office of judges. We are therefore going to see the government template: Protocol No 14 on the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms on the modification of the Convention's control system.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-12-07-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00132_d7e20555	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) My colleague Christoph Matznetter and I were on 28 May. In September, a request was filed with the aim of achieving a change in the taxation of repayments in insolvency proceedings. I'm going to give you an example so you know what it's all about. An operation is in bankruptcy in 2003. Payments of payments, i.e. the wages of workers, by the insolvency fund are usually made half a or three-quarters of a year later. If, in the meantime, one employee or another employee has already started a new employment relationship again, in 2004, then the tax charge shall be as follows: Repayments from 2003 and income from 2004 will be counted together, and that means that the basis of assessment is higher. We wanted to change that. Our proposal was: Repayments in insolvency proceedings shall be deemed to be inflows in the calendar year for which the claim exists. I would like to join the government or the Ministry of Finance Thank you for taking our request into the government paper, so that we could achieve a relief for the workers. - Thank you.
